What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what brings me in is... I just can't keep my mind on one thing anymore. It's getting really frustrating. If I'm working and one saw starts up in the shop, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I just get a sudden thought about another job, it completely pulls me off what I’m doing. And then, before I know it, the whole day just gets away from me. I really need to figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. Usually, I'm just trying to keep up with what everyone else is saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when doing things in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think anyone actually enjoys waiting in a long line at the grocery store or the lumber yard, but... I don't have a problem doing it. I just wait my turn like everyone else. It's never really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say that, no. I mean, in my line of work, you really have to finish what you start. A customer isn't going to pay for half a cabinet or an unfinished table. I might get a bit sidetracked while I'm working on it, or bounce between a couple of different pieces in the shop, but... I always get the job done eventually. So, no, finishing things isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I really do. It's gotten a lot worse lately since I've been stuck doing the shop paperwork instead of just building. I just... I can't seem to figure out what needs doing first. It's a real mess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what brings me in is... I just can't keep my mind on one thing anymore. It's getting really frustrating. If I'm working and one saw starts up in the shop, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I just get a sudden thought about another job, it completely pulls me off what I’m doing. And then, before I know it, the whole day just gets away from me. I really need to figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. Usually, I'm just trying to keep up with what everyone else is saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when doing things in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think anyone actually enjoys waiting in a long line at the grocery store or the lumber yard, but... I don't have a problem doing it. I just wait my turn like everyone else. It's never really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say that, no. I mean, in my line of work, you really have to finish what you start. A customer isn't going to pay for half a cabinet or an unfinished table. I might get a bit sidetracked while I'm working on it, or bounce between a couple of different pieces in the shop, but... I always get the job done eventually. So, no, finishing things isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I really do. It's gotten a lot worse lately since I've been stuck doing the shop paperwork instead of just building. I just... I can't seem to figure out what needs doing first. So I've got bids, schedules, invoices, and all the questions from the apprentices just piling up on my workbench. It gets to the point where I'm rushing to catch up, and I'm still missing deadlines. It's a real mess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes, especially with all this paperwork piling up, but... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I keep a pretty level head for the most part. I'm not snapping at people or crying out of nowhere or anything like that. So, yeah, it's taking a pretty big toll on everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. Honestly, it's usually the exact opposite. I'm... I'm more likely to get distracted by every little noise or movement around the shop than I am to tune it out. I can't really think of a time where I was ever that focused on just one thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with restlessness and fidgeting as a child, before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really remember being restless or fidgety like that when I was a kid. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. If I'm working and one saw starts up in the shop, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I just get a sudden thought about another job, it completely pulls me off what I’m doing. And then, before I know it, the whole day just gets away from me. I really need to figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. Usually, I'm just trying to keep up with what everyone else is saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when doing things in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think anyone actually enjoys waiting in a long line at the grocery store or the lumber yard, but... I don't have a problem doing it. I just wait my turn like everyone else. It's never really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say that, no. I mean, in my line of work, you really have to finish what you start. A customer isn't going to pay for half a cabinet or an unfinished table. I might get a bit sidetracked while I'm working on it, or bounce between a couple of different pieces in the shop, but... I always get the job done eventually. So, no, finishing things isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I really do. It's gotten a lot worse lately since I've been stuck doing the shop paperwork instead of just building. I just... I can't seem to figure out what needs doing first. So I've got bids, schedules, invoices, and all the questions from the apprentices just piling up on my workbench. It gets to the point where I'm rushing to catch up, and I'm still missing deadlines. It's a real mess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes, especially with all this paperwork piling up, but... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I keep a pretty level head for the most part. I'm not snapping at people or crying out of nowhere or anything like that. Just regular, everyday frustration, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble paying attention and sitting still, or is this something that has developed more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely not a new thing. Even way back in grade school, you know, before I was 12, I had the same issues with paying attention. My teachers were always telling my mother that I was a bright kid, but just... careless. I was always losing my homework, or I'd completely forget the instructions they just gave the class. And instead of finishing my seatwork, I'd just be staring out the window. So, yeah, the trouble paying attention has been there for as long as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or impairment do these symptoms cause you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's causing a lot of problems, honestly. At the shop, my mistakes and the delays... they've actually cost me commissions. We've had to do rework on jobs because I messed up, and the crew is getting to where they're less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering materials. It's tough to see them lose trust in me like that. \n\nAnd it's not just at work, either. At home, my husband has basically had to take over paying the bills and keeping track of all our reminders. I just keep misplacing important papers or completely missing what needs to get done. So, yeah, it's taking a pretty big toll on everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. Honestly, it's usually the exact opposite. I'm... I'm more likely to get distracted by every little noise or movement around the shop than I am to tune it out. I can't really think of a time where I was ever that focused on just one thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with restlessness and fidgeting as a child, before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really remember being restless or fidgety like that when I was a kid. Like I said before, it was mostly just the spacing out and losing things. I was more the kid staring out the window, not the one bouncing off the walls.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with paying attention and restlessness in social situations, like when you're with friends or at family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, the restlessness isn't really an issue for me at all. And as for paying attention when I'm around friends or at family gatherings... I haven't really noticed it being a problem. I mean, maybe I zone out a little sometimes, but I can't say it's something that stands out or causes trouble like it does at the shop or with the bills at home. So, no, I'm not really sure it affects me much in those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have trouble paying attention or get restless in multiple places, such as at work, at home, and in other settings outside of those?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I don't really get restless anywhere. But for the paying attention part... it's really just at the shop and at home. It definitely affects my work, and it messes me up when I try to sit down and do the bills at the house. But outside of those two places? No, I haven't really noticed it being an issue anywhere else.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention or behaving in school when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Even back in grade school, well before I was 12, it was an issue. I remember my teachers were always telling my mother that I was bright but just... careless. I was always losing my homework or forgetting what the instructions were. And like I mentioned earlier, I was the one staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork. So yeah, paying attention was definitely a problem back then too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble paying attention in quiet activities like reading or playing games when you're by yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, it's not so much the paying attention part when things are quiet, but... sitting still. Even at home, or when I'm at church and it's supposed to be quiet, I just can't seem to settle. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in my seat, whispering to my husband, or just getting up for no real reason. Sitting still in the quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If I'm working and one saw starts up in the shop, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I just get a sudden thought about another job, it completely pulls me off what I’m doing. And then, before I know it, the whole day just gets away from me. I really need to figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. Usually, I'm just trying to keep up with what everyone else is saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when doing things in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think anyone actually enjoys waiting in a long line at the grocery store or the lumber yard, but... I don't have a problem doing it. I just wait my turn like everyone else. It's never really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say that, no. I mean, in my line of work, you really have to finish what you start. A customer isn't going to pay for half a cabinet or an unfinished table. I might get a bit sidetracked while I'm working on it, or bounce between a couple of different pieces in the shop, but... I always get the job done eventually. So, no, finishing things isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I really do. It's gotten a lot worse lately since I've been stuck doing the shop paperwork instead of just building. I just... I can't seem to figure out what needs doing first. So I've got bids, schedules, invoices, and all the questions from the apprentices just piling up on my workbench. It gets to the point where I'm rushing to catch up, and I'm still missing deadlines. It's a real mess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes, especially with all this paperwork piling up, but... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I keep a pretty level head for the most part. I'm not snapping at people or crying out of nowhere or anything like that. Just regular, everyday frustration, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble paying attention and sitting still, or is this something that has developed more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely not a new thing. Even way back in grade school, you know, before I was 12, I had the same issues with paying attention. My teachers were always telling my mother that I was a bright kid, but just... careless. I was always losing my homework, or I'd completely forget the instructions they just gave the class. And instead of finishing my seatwork, I'd just be staring out the window. So, yeah, the trouble paying attention has been there for as long as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or impairment do these symptoms cause you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's causing a lot of problems, honestly. At the shop, my mistakes and the delays... they've actually cost me commissions. We've had to do rework on jobs because I messed up, and the crew is getting to where they're less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering materials. It's tough to see them lose trust in me like that. \n\nAnd it's not just at work, either. At home, my husband has basically had to take over paying the bills and keeping track of all our reminders. I just keep misplacing important papers or completely missing what needs to get done. So, yeah, it's taking a pretty big toll on everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. Honestly, it's usually the exact opposite. I'm... I'm more likely to get distracted by every little noise or movement around the shop than I am to tune it out. I can't really think of a time where I was ever that focused on just one thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with restlessness and fidgeting as a child, before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really remember being restless or fidgety like that when I was a kid. Like I said before, it was mostly just the spacing out and losing things. I was more the kid staring out the window, not the one bouncing off the walls.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with paying attention and restlessness in social situations, like when you're with friends or at family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, the restlessness isn't really an issue for me at all. And as for paying attention when I'm around friends or at family gatherings... I haven't really noticed it being a problem. I mean, maybe I zone out a little sometimes, but I can't say it's something that stands out or causes trouble like it does at the shop or with the bills at home. So, no, I'm not really sure it affects me much in those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have trouble paying attention or get restless in multiple places, such as at work, at home, and in other settings outside of those?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I don't really get restless anywhere. But for the paying attention part... it's really just at the shop and at home. It definitely affects my work, and it messes me up when I try to sit down and do the bills at the house. But outside of those two places? No, I haven't really noticed it being an issue anywhere else.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention or behaving in school when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Even back in grade school, well before I was 12, it was an issue. I remember my teachers were always telling my mother that I was bright but just... careless. I was always losing my homework or forgetting what the instructions were. And like I mentioned earlier, I was the one staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork. Sitting still in the quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced problems with paying attention or restlessness in at least two different settings, such as at work and at home?\n\nPatient: I think we might have just covered this a minute ago, but yes. Like I was saying, the trouble focusing is definitely an issue at the shop and at the house. And the trouble sitting still... well, that happens at home and at church. So yes, it's definitely affecting me in a few different places.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what brings me in is... I just can't keep my mind on one thing anymore. It's getting really frustrating. If I'm working and one saw starts up in the shop, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I just get a sudden thought about another job, it completely pulls me off what I’m doing. And then, before I know it, the whole day just gets away from me. I really need to figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or if I'm stuck sitting through a long meeting... I just feel really wound up inside. It's like I have this strong need to get up and move around. Even though my wrist is healed now and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish my work, I just get so restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I might get pulled away by a distraction, like I mentioned, but I don't just jump into things without thinking them through. In my line of work, you kind of have to plan before you make a cut, you know? So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with acting on impulse. It's really just keeping my mind on the task that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or in other situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just at the shop. Even at home, or... well, like when we're in church, and things are supposed to be quiet. I catch myself tapping my foot, shifting around in the seat, whispering to my husband... or I'll just get up for no real reason. Sitting still when it's quiet like that... it just feels almost unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I can talk your ear off if we're on a subject I know well, like woodworking, but I don't just talk to hear myself talk. I wouldn't say I take over the conversation or anything like that. Usually, I'm just trying to keep up with what everyone else is saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when doing things in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think anyone actually enjoys waiting in a long line at the grocery store or the lumber yard, but... I don't have a problem doing it. I just wait my turn like everyone else. It's never really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say that, no. I mean, in my line of work, you really have to finish what you start. A customer isn't going to pay for half a cabinet or an unfinished table. I might get a bit sidetracked while I'm working on it, or bounce between a couple of different pieces in the shop, but... I always get the job done eventually. So, no, finishing things isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I really do. It's gotten a lot worse lately since I've been stuck doing the shop paperwork instead of just building. I just... I can't seem to figure out what needs doing first. So I've got bids, schedules, invoices, and all the questions from the apprentices just piling up on my workbench. It gets to the point where I'm rushing to catch up, and I'm still missing deadlines. It's a real mess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes, especially with all this paperwork piling up, but... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I keep a pretty level head for the most part. I'm not snapping at people or crying out of nowhere or anything like that. Just regular, everyday frustration, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble paying attention and sitting still, or is this something that has developed more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely not a new thing. Even way back in grade school, you know, before I was 12, I had the same issues with paying attention. My teachers were always telling my mother that I was a bright kid, but just... careless. I was always losing my homework, or I'd completely forget the instructions they just gave the class. And instead of finishing my seatwork, I'd just be staring out the window. So, yeah, the trouble paying attention has been there for as long as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or impairment do these symptoms cause you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's causing a lot of problems, honestly. At the shop, my mistakes and the delays... they've actually cost me commissions. We've had to do rework on jobs because I messed up, and the crew is getting to where they're less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering materials. It's tough to see them lose trust in me like that. \n\nAnd it's not just at work, either. At home, my husband has basically had to take over paying the bills and keeping track of all our reminders. I just keep misplacing important papers or completely missing what needs to get done. So, yeah, it's taking a pretty big toll on everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. Honestly, it's usually the exact opposite. I'm... I'm more likely to get distracted by every little noise or movement around the shop than I am to tune it out. I can't really think of a time where I was ever that focused on just one thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with restlessness and fidgeting as a child, before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really remember being restless or fidgety like that when I was a kid. Like I said before, it was mostly just the spacing out and losing things. I was more the kid staring out the window, not the one bouncing off the walls.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with paying attention and restlessness in social situations, like when you're with friends or at family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, the restlessness isn't really an issue for me at all. And as for paying attention when I'm around friends or at family gatherings... I haven't really noticed it being a problem. I mean, maybe I zone out a little sometimes, but I can't say it's something that stands out or causes trouble like it does at the shop or with the bills at home. So, no, I'm not really sure it affects me much in those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have trouble paying attention or get restless in multiple places, such as at work, at home, and in other settings outside of those?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I don't really get restless anywhere. But for the paying attention part... it's really just at the shop and at home. It definitely affects my work, and it messes me up when I try to sit down and do the bills at the house. But outside of those two places? I was always losing my homework or forgetting what the instructions were. And like I mentioned earlier, I was the one staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork. So yeah, paying attention was definitely a problem back then too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble paying attention in quiet activities like reading or playing games when you're by yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, it's not so much the paying attention part when things are quiet, but... sitting still. Like I was saying, the trouble focusing is definitely an issue at the shop and at the house. And the trouble sitting still... well, that happens at home and at church. So yes, it's definitely affecting me in a few different places.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever diagnosed with attention deficit hyperactivity disorder as a child?\n\nPatient: No, I never was. Back when I was in school... well, I don't think they really looked for that kind of thing, you know? Like I said, they just told my mom I was careless or not applying myself. Yes, I think we kind of just went over that. Like I was saying a minute ago, I definitely get restless and fidgety in a few different places. It happens at home, at church when I'm supposed to be quiet, and at work in the shop, too. So, yes, it's definitely in multiple situations.
